The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 6 is 5.00. The other faces contribute 5.00 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>5.00&minus;5.00 =</span> <strong>0</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(5.00+5.00)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>1.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>5.00+0 =</span> <strong>5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>5.00+5.00 =</span> <strong>10</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 1?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(16&minus;8)/8 =</span> <strong>1</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(16&minus;8)<sup>2</sup>/16 =</span> <strong>4</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(16&minus;8)<sup>2</sup>/8 =</span> <strong>8</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(16&minus;8)<sup>2</sup>/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>10.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(16&minus;8)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>64</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 5 is 6.00. The other faces contribute 6.00 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>6.00&minus;6.00 =</span> <strong>0</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(6.00+6.00)/6 =</span> <strong>2</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>6.00+0 =</span> <strong>6</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>6.00+6.00 =</span> <strong>12</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 2?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(20&minus;10)/10 =</span> <strong>1</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(20&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/20 =</span> <strong>5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(20&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/10 =</span> <strong>10</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(20&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>16.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(20&minus;10)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>100</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 3 is 6.00. The other faces contribute 6.00 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>6.00&minus;6.00 =</span> <strong>0</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(6.00+6.00)/6 =</span> <strong>2</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>6.00+0 =</span> <strong>6</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>6.00+6.00 =</span> <strong>12</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 1 is 0.90. The other faces contribute 4.90 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.90+4.90)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.97</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>4.90&minus;0.90 =</span> <strong>4</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>4.90+0 =</span> <strong>4.9</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.90+4.90 =</span> <strong>5.8</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 5 is 15.00. The other faces contribute 15.00 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>15.00&minus;15.00 =</span> <strong>0</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(15.00+15.00)/6 =</span> <strong>5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>15.00+0 =</span> <strong>15</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>15.00+15.00 =</span> <strong>30</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 2 is 3.20. The other faces contribute 8.40 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(3.20+8.40)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>1.93</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>8.40&minus;3.20 =</span> <strong>5.2</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>8.40+0 =</span> <strong>8.4</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>3.20+8.40 =</span> <strong>11.6</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 2 is 10.00. The other faces contribute 10.00 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>10.00&minus;10.00 =</span> <strong>0</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(10.00+10.00)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>3.33</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>10.00+0 =</span> <strong>10</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>10.00+10.00 =</span> <strong>20</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 2?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(21&minus;20)<sup>2</sup>/20 =</span> <strong>0.05</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(21&minus;20)<sup>2</sup>/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.17</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(21&minus;20)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>1</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 3?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(8&minus;10)/10 =</span> <strong>-0.2</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(8&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/10 =</span> <strong>0.4</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(8&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/8 =</span> <strong>0.5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(8&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(8&minus;10)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>4</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 5?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(30&minus;15)/15 =</span> <strong>1</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(30&minus;15)<sup>2</sup>/30 =</span> <strong>7.5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(30&minus;15)<sup>2</sup>/15 =</span> <strong>15</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(30&minus;15)<sup>2</sup>/6 =</span> <strong>37.5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(30&minus;15)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>225</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 3 is 0.33. The other faces contribute 0.50 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.33+0.50)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.14</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.50&minus;0.33 =</span> <strong>0.17</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.50+0 =</span> <strong>0.5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.33+0.50 =</span> <strong>0.83</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 1?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(22&minus;20)/20 =</span> <strong>0.1</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(22&minus;20)<sup>2</sup>/22 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.18</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(22&minus;20)<sup>2</sup>/20 =</span> <strong>0.2</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(22&minus;20)<sup>2</sup>/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(22&minus;20)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>4</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 5?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(13&minus;15)/15 &asymp;</span> <strong>-0.13</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(13&minus;15)<sup>2</sup>/15 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.27</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(13&minus;15)<sup>2</sup>/13 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.31</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(13&minus;15)<sup>2</sup>/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(13&minus;15)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>4</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 6?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(3&minus;5)/5 =</span> <strong>-0.4</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(3&minus;5)<sup>2</sup>/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(3&minus;5)<sup>2</sup>/5 =</span> <strong>0.8</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(3&minus;5)<sup>2</sup>/3 &asymp;</span> <strong>1.33</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(3&minus;5)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>4</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 2?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(22&minus;20)/20 =</span> <strong>0.1</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(22&minus;20)<sup>2</sup>/22 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.18</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(22&minus;20)<sup>2</sup>/20 =</span> <strong>0.2</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(22&minus;20)<sup>2</sup>/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(22&minus;20)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>4</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 4?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(7&minus;5)/5 =</span> <strong>0.4</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(7&minus;5)<sup>2</sup>/7 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.57</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(7&minus;5)<sup>2</sup>/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(7&minus;5)<sup>2</sup>/5 =</span> <strong>0.8</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(7&minus;5)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>4</strong>	Incorrect
